\displaystyle\qquad+\biggl(\frac{3\pi^{2}\theta_{2}^{*}}{(\sigma^{*})^{2/\alpha}}\biggr)^{\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}(\lambda_{1,1}^{*}+\theta_{0}^{*})\partial G\bigl((\sigma_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\sigma_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\bigr)\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\sigma}_{1,1}^{2}-(\sigma_{1,1}^{*})^{2}\\
\hat{\sigma}_{1,2}^{2}-(\sigma_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\end{pmatrix}+o_{p}(1).

Therefore, setting

\boldsymbol{\vartheta}^{*}=(\theta_{0}^{*},\boldsymbol{\vartheta}_{-1}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}
and

\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=(1,0,0,0,0)^{\mathsf{T}}
, we obtain from (
5.55
)
that under

\frac{n^{2-\alpha}}{mN^{2\gamma}}\to 0
and

\frac{n^{2-\alpha+\epsilon}}{M_{1}^{2\epsilon}\land M_{2}^{2\epsilon}}\to 0
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\theta}_{0}-\theta_{0}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\\
\hat{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}^{*}\\
\hat{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{*}\\
\hat{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\Biggl\{\biggl(\frac{3\pi^{2}\theta_{2}^{*}}{(\sigma^{*})^{2/\alpha}}\biggr)^{\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}\boldsymbol{\vartheta}^{*}\partial G((\sigma_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\sigma_{1,2}^{*})^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\biggl(\frac{3\pi^{2}\theta_{2}^{*}}{(\sigma^{*})^{2/\alpha}}\biggr)^{\frac{\alpha}{1-\alpha}}\lambda_{1,1}^{*}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\partial G((\sigma_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\sigma_{1,2}^{*})^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad-\biggl(\frac{3\pi^{2}\theta_{2}^{*}}{(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\biggr)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\partial H((\sigma_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\sigma_{1,2}^{*})^{2})\Biggr\}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\sigma}_{1,1}^{2}-(\sigma_{1,1}^{*})^{2}\\
\hat{\sigma}_{1,2}^{2}-(\sigma_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\end{pmatrix}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=:A\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\sigma}_{1,1}^{2}-(\sigma_{1,1}^{*})^{2}\\
\hat{\sigma}_{1,2}^{2}-(\sigma_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\end{pmatrix}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\longrightarrow}}N(0,J),

where

\displaystyle J=A\begin{pmatrix}2(\sigma_{1,1}^{*})^{4}&0\\
0&2(\sigma_{1,2}^{*})^{4}\end{pmatrix}A^{\mathsf{T}}.

Let

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{a}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\lambda_{1,1}^{*}}{3\pi^{2}(1-\alpha)\theta_{2}^{*}}\boldsymbol{\vartheta}^{*}+\frac{\lambda_{1,1}^{*}\lambda_{1,2}^{*}}{3\pi^{2}\alpha\theta_{2}^{*}}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=:r_{1}\boldsymbol{\vartheta}^{*}+r_{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{1},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{a}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{1,2}^{*}}{3\pi^{2}(1-\alpha)\theta_{2}^{*}}\boldsymbol{\vartheta}^{*}-\frac{\lambda_{1,1}^{*}\lambda_{1,2}^{*}}{3\pi^{2}\alpha\theta_{2}^{*}}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=:r_{3}\boldsymbol{\vartheta}^{*}-r_{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}.

Note that

\displaystyle\partial G((\sigma_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\sigma_{1,2}^{*})^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{(\sigma^{*})^{\frac{2\alpha}{1-\alpha}}}{(1-\alpha)(3\pi^{2}\theta_{2}^{*})^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}}\bigl(-(\lambda_{1,1}^{*})^{1+\alpha},(\lambda_{1,2}^{*})^{1+\alpha}\bigr),

\displaystyle\partial H((\sigma_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\sigma_{1,2}^{*})^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{(\sigma^{*})^{\frac{2\alpha}{1-\alpha}}}{\alpha(1-\alpha)(3\pi^{2}\theta_{2}^{*})^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}+1}}\bigl(-(\lambda_{1,1}^{*})^{1+\alpha}\bigl(\alpha\lambda_{1,1}^{*}+(1-\alpha)\lambda_{1,2}^{*}\bigr),\lambda_{1,1}^{*}(\lambda_{1,2}^{*})^{1+\alpha}\bigr),

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3\pi^{2}(1-\alpha)\theta_{2}^{*}(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\boldsymbol{\vartheta}^{*}\bigl(-(\lambda_{1,1}^{*})^{1+\alpha},(\lambda_{1,2}^{*})^{1+\alpha}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{3\pi^{2}\alpha\theta_{2}^{*}(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\bigl((\lambda_{1,1}^{*})^{1+\alpha}\lambda_{1,2}^{*},-\lambda_{1,1}^{*}(\lambda_{1,2}^{*})^{1+\alpha}\bigr)

\displaystyle=((\sigma_{1,1}^{*})^{-2}\boldsymbol{a}_{1},(\sigma_{1,2}^{*})^{-2}\boldsymbol{a}_{2})

and
J=2(\boldsymbol{a}_{1}\boldsymbol{a}_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}+\boldsymbol{a}_{2}\boldsymbol{a}_{2}^{\mathsf{T}})
.
Since

r_{1}\theta_{0}^{*}+r_{2}=\frac{\lambda_{1,1}^{*}}{3\pi^{2}\theta_{2}^{*}}\biggl(\frac{\lambda_{1,2}^{*}}{\alpha}-\frac{\theta_{0}^{*}}{1-\alpha}\biggr),\quad r_{3}\theta_{0}^{*}-r_{2}=\frac{-\lambda_{1,2}^{*}}{3\pi^{2}\theta_{2}^{*}}\biggl(\frac{\lambda_{1,1}^{*}}{\alpha}-\frac{\theta_{0}^{*}}{1-\alpha}\biggr),

\displaystyle(r_{1}\theta_{0}^{*}+r_{2})^{2}+(r_{3}\theta_{0}^{*}-r_{2})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{9\pi^{4}(\theta_{2}^{*})^{2}}\Biggl\{(\lambda_{1,1}^{*})^{2}\biggl(\frac{\lambda_{1,2}^{*}}{\alpha}-\frac{\theta_{0}^{*}}{1-\alpha}\biggr)^{2}+(\lambda_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\biggl(\frac{\lambda_{1,1}^{*}}{\alpha}-\frac{\theta_{0}^{*}}{1-\alpha}\biggr)^{2}\Biggr\}=\frac{c_{1}}{9\pi^{4}(\theta_{2}^{*})^{2}},

\displaystyle r_{1}(r_{1}\theta_{0}^{*}+r_{2})+r_{3}(r_{3}\theta_{0}^{*}-r_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{9\pi^{4}(1-\alpha)(\theta_{2}^{*})^{2}}\Biggl\{(\lambda_{1,1}^{*})^{2}\biggl(\frac{\lambda_{1,2}^{*}}{\alpha}-\frac{\theta_{0}^{*}}{1-\alpha}\biggr)+(\lambda_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\biggl(\frac{\lambda_{1,1}^{*}}{\alpha}-\frac{\theta_{0}^{*}}{1-\alpha}\biggr)\Biggr\}=\frac{c_{2}}{9\pi^{4}(\theta_{2}^{*})^{2}},

\displaystyle r_{1}^{2}+r_{3}^{2}=\frac{1}{9\pi^{4}(1-\alpha)^{2}(\theta_{2}^{*})^{2}}\bigl\{(\lambda_{1,1}^{*})^{2}+(\lambda_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\bigr\}=\frac{c_{3}}{9\pi^{4}(\theta_{2}^{*})^{2}},

we see that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{a}_{1}\boldsymbol{a}_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}+\boldsymbol{a}_{2}\boldsymbol{a}_{2}^{\mathsf{T}}=\frac{1}{9\pi^{4}(\theta_{2}^{*})^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}c_{1}&c_{2}(\boldsymbol{\vartheta}_{-1}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}\\
c_{2}\boldsymbol{\vartheta}_{-1}^{*}&c_{3}\boldsymbol{\vartheta}_{-1}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\vartheta}_{-1}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}\end{pmatrix}

and thus we obtain the desired result.

(1) For the evaluation of
\mathcal{A}_{n}
,
the following holds by the same argument as above.
For a sequence
\{r_{n}\}
with

\frac{r_{n}n^{1-\alpha}}{mN^{2\gamma}}\to 0

and
\frac{r_{n}n^{1-\alpha+\epsilon}}{M_{1}^{2\epsilon}\land M_{2}^{2\epsilon}}\to 0

for some
\epsilon<\alpha
as
n\to\infty
and
M\to\infty
,

(5.56)

r_{n}\mathcal{A}_{n}=o_{p}(1).

Therefore, it follows from (
5.56
) that
under
\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{mN^{2\gamma}}\to 0

and
\frac{n^{1-\alpha+\epsilon}}{M_{1}^{2\epsilon}\land M_{2}^{2\epsilon}}\to 0
,

\mathcal{A}_{n}=o_{p}(1)
,
and from (
5.53
) and (
5.54
), we obtain that

\hat{\sigma}_{1,2}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\sigma_{1,1}^{*})^{2}
,

\hat{\sigma}_{1,2}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\sigma_{1,2}^{*})^{2}

and then

(\hat{\theta}_{0},\hat{\theta}_{1},\hat{\eta}_{1},\hat{\theta}_{2},\hat{\sigma}^{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\theta_{0}^{*},\theta_{1}^{*},\eta_{1}^{*},\theta_{2}^{*},(\sigma^{*})^{2}).

∎

5.3.
Proofs of Proposition
3.4
, Theorems
3.5
and
3.6

For
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta}
, choose two functions

g_{1},g_{2}:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying the following conditions.

(c)

f(s)=g_{1}(s)g_{2}(s)
and
g_{2}(as)=g_{2}(a)g_{2}(s)
,
a,s>0
.

(d)

sg_{1}^{\prime}g_{2}(s^{2})\boldsymbol{1}_{[1,\infty)}
,

sg_{1}g_{2}^{\prime}(s^{2})\boldsymbol{1}_{[1,\infty)}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
.

For
f=g_{1}g_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta}
with (c) and (d), we define

\widetilde{R}_{1,N}=O\Biggl(\Delta_{N}\biggl(\int_{\Delta_{N}^{1/2}}^{1}s|g_{1}^{\prime}g_{2}(s^{2})|\mathrm{d}s\lor\int_{\Delta_{N}^{1/2}}^{1}s|g_{1}g_{2}^{\prime}(s^{2})|\mathrm{d}s\lor\int_{\Delta_{N}^{1/2}}^{1}s^{3}|f^{\prime\prime}(s^{2})|\mathrm{d}s\biggr)\Biggr).

As an extension of Lemma
5.1
, the following lemma holds.

If
f=g_{1}g_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{\beta}
satisfies (c) and (d), then the following hold.

(1)

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1}(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})g_{2}(\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})\\
=\frac{1}{4\pi\theta_{2}g_{2}(\theta_{2})}\int_{0}^{\infty}f(s)\mathrm{d}s-\frac{1}{g_{2}(\theta_{2})}\int_{D_{N}^{(1)}\cup D_{N}^{(2)}}f(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|x|^{2})\mathrm{d}x+O(\widetilde{R}_{1,N}\lor\Delta_{N}^{1-\beta/2})
.

(2)

For any
y,z\in[\delta,1-\delta]
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1}(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})g_{2}(\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})\cos(2\pi k_{1}y)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{g_{2}(\theta_{2})}\int_{D_{N}^{(1)}}f(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|x|^{2})\mathrm{d}x+O\biggl(\frac{\widetilde{R}_{1,N}\lor R_{2,N}}{\delta^{2}}\biggr),

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1}(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})g_{2}(\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})\cos(2\pi k_{2}z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{g_{2}(\theta_{2})}\int_{D_{N}^{(2)}}f(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|x|^{2})\mathrm{d}x+O\biggl(\frac{\widetilde{R}_{1,N}\lor R_{2,N}}{\delta^{2}}\biggr).

(3)

For any
y,z\in[\delta,1-\delta]
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1}(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})g_{2}(\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})\cos(2\pi k_{1}y)\cos(2\pi k_{2}z)=O\biggl(\frac{\widetilde{R}_{1,N}\lor R_{2,N}\lor\Delta_{N}^{1-\beta/2}}{\delta^{3}}\biggr).

Furthermore, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{D_{N}^{(1)}\cup D_{N}^{(2)}}f(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|x|^{2})\mathrm{d}x=O(R_{N}^{(\beta)}).

Proof.

In a similar way to (
5.2
), if (d) holds, then it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{F_{N}}|g_{1}^{\prime}g_{2}(|x|^{2})|\mathrm{d}x\lor\int_{F_{N}}|g_{1}g_{2}^{\prime}(|x|^{2})|\mathrm{d}x=O\biggl(\int_{\Delta_{N}^{1/2}}^{1}s|g_{1}^{\prime}g_{2}(s^{2})|\mathrm{d}s\lor\int_{\Delta_{N}^{1/2}}^{1}s|g_{1}g_{2}^{\prime}(s^{2})|\mathrm{d}s\biggr).

By the Taylor expansion, it follows that

\displaystyle\Biggl|\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1}(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})g_{2}(\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})-\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1}(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})g_{2}(\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})\Biggr|

\displaystyle=\Biggl|\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}\biggl\{g_{1}(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})g_{2}(\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{1}g_{1}^{\prime}\bigl(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N}+u(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}-\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2})\Delta_{N}\bigr)g_{2}(\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})\mathrm{d}u\Bigl(\frac{\theta_{1}^{2}+\eta_{1}^{2}}{4\theta_{2}}-\theta_{0}\Bigr)\Delta_{N}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{1}g_{1}(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})g_{2}^{\prime}\bigl(\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N}+u(\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}-\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2})\Delta_{N}\bigr)\mathrm{d}u\,\mu_{0}\Delta_{N}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1}(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})g_{2}(\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})\Biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|g_{1}^{\prime}\bigl(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N}+u(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}-\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2})\Delta_{N}\bigr)g_{2}(\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})\bigr|\mathrm{d}u\Bigl|\frac{\theta_{1}^{2}+\eta_{1}^{2}}{4\theta_{2}}-\theta_{0}\Bigr|\Delta_{N}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\bigl|g_{1}(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})g_{2}^{\prime}\bigl(\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N}+u(\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}-\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2})\Delta_{N}\bigr)\bigr|\mathrm{d}u|\mu_{0}|\Delta_{N}

(5.57)

\displaystyle=O\Biggl(\Delta_{N}\biggl(\int_{F_{N}}|g_{1}^{\prime}g_{2}(|x|^{2})|\mathrm{d}x\lor\int_{F_{N}}|g_{1}g_{2}^{\prime}(|x|^{2})|\mathrm{d}x\biggr)\Biggr)=O(\widetilde{R}_{1,N}),

and from (c), we obtain that

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1}(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})g_{2}(\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta_{N}}{g_{2}(\theta_{2})}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1}(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})g_{2}(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta_{N}}{g_{2}(\theta_{2})}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}f(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N}).

In a similar way to the proof of Lemma
5.1
,
it holds from (
5.57
) that

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}f(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})=\frac{1}{4\pi\theta_{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}f(s)\mathrm{d}s-\int_{D_{N}^{(1)}\cup D_{N}^{(2)}}f(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|x|^{2})\mathrm{d}x+O(\widetilde{R}_{1,N}\lor\Delta_{N}^{1-\beta/2})

and therefore we obtain (1).
Similarly, it holds that

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1}(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})g_{2}(\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})\cos(2\pi k_{1}y)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta_{N}}{g_{2}(\theta_{2})}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}f(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})\cos(2\pi k_{1}y)+O(\widetilde{R}_{1,N}),

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1}(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})g_{2}(\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})\cos(2\pi k_{1}y)\cos(2\pi k_{2}z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta_{N}}{g_{2}(\theta_{2})}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}f(\theta_{2}\pi^{2}|\boldsymbol{k}|^{2}\Delta_{N})\cos(2\pi k_{1}y)\cos(2\pi k_{2}z)+O(\widetilde{R}_{1,N}),

and therefore from Lemma
5.1
, we obtain (2) and (3).
∎

The functions
f_{\alpha}
and
f_{\alpha,\tau}
in (
5.26
)
can be expressed as

f_{\alpha}(s)=\frac{1-\mathrm{e}^{-s}}{s^{1+\alpha}}=\frac{1-\mathrm{e}^{-s}}{s}\cdot\frac{1}{s^{\alpha}}=:g_{1}(s)g_{2,\alpha}(s),

f_{\alpha,\tau}(s)=\frac{(1-\mathrm{e}^{-s})^{2}}{s^{1+\alpha}}\mathrm{e}^{-\tau s}=\frac{(1-\mathrm{e}^{-s})^{2}}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\tau s}\cdot\frac{1}{s^{\alpha}}=:g_{1,\tau}(s)g_{2,\alpha}(s),

and satisfy (c) and (d). Hence, it follows
from Lemma
5.5
and an analogous proof of Lemma
5.2
that

(5.58)

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1}(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})g_{2,\alpha}(\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})e_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{2}(y,z)=\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{4\pi\alpha\theta_{2}^{1-\alpha}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}y}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}z}+O(\delta^{-3}\Delta_{N}^{1-\alpha}),

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}g_{1,\tau}(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})g_{2,\alpha}(\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})e_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{2}(y,z)

(5.59)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{4\pi\alpha\theta_{2}^{1-\alpha}}\bigl(-\tau^{\alpha}+2(1+\tau)^{\alpha}-(2+\tau)^{\alpha}\bigr)\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}y}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}z}+O(\delta^{-3}\Delta_{N}^{1-\alpha}),

(5.60)

\displaystyle\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}\frac{1-\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N}}}{\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{N}^{1+\alpha}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}f_{\alpha}(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})\biggl(\frac{\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}}{\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}}\biggr)^{\alpha}=O(\Delta_{N}^{\alpha}),

(5.61)

\displaystyle\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}\frac{(1-\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N}})^{2}}{\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{N}^{1+\alpha}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}f_{\alpha,0}(\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N})\biggl(\frac{\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}}{\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}}\biggr)^{\alpha}=O(\Delta_{N}^{\alpha}).

Set

\displaystyle B_{1,i,\boldsymbol{k}}^{Q_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sigma(1-\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Delta_{N}})}{\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\alpha/2}}\int_{0}^{(i-1)\Delta_{N}}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}((i-1)\Delta_{N}-s)}\mathrm{d}w_{\boldsymbol{k}}(s),

\displaystyle B_{2,i,\boldsymbol{k}}^{Q_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma}{\mu_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\alpha/2}}\int_{(i-1)\Delta_{N}}^{i\Delta_{N}}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}(i\Delta_{N}-s)}\mathrm{d}w_{\boldsymbol{k}}(s),

and
B_{i,\boldsymbol{k}}^{Q_{2}}=B_{1,i,\boldsymbol{k}}^{Q_{2}}+B_{2,i,\boldsymbol{k}}^{Q_{2}}
.
The increment
\Delta_{i}x_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{Q_{2}}
can be expressed as

\Delta_{i}x_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{Q_{2}}=A_{i,\boldsymbol{k}}+B_{i,\boldsymbol{k}}^{Q_{2}}
.

From (
5.58
)-(
5.61
),
the following lemma holds by an analogous proof of Lemma
5.4
.

It holds that uniformly in
(y,z)\in D_{\delta}
,

\sum_{\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{\ell}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[B_{i,\boldsymbol{k}}^{Q_{2}}B_{i,\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{Q_{2}}]e_{\boldsymbol{k}}(y,z)e_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}(y,z)=\Delta_{N}^{\alpha}\frac{\sigma^{2}\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{4\pi\alpha\theta_{2}^{1-\alpha}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}y}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\eta_{1}}{\theta_{2}}z}+O(\Delta_{N}).

Moreover, it holds that uniformly in
(y,z)\in D_{\delta}
,

\sup_{i\neq j}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{\ell}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}[B_{i,\boldsymbol{k}}^{Q_{2}}B_{j,\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{Q_{2}}]e_{\boldsymbol{k}}(y,z)e_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}(y,z)\Bigr|=O(1).

Proof of Proposition
3.4
.

It can be shown in the same way as the proof of Proposition
3.1

by using Lemmas
5.3
and
5.6
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.5
.

It is shown similarly to the proof of Theorem
3.2
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.6
.

(a) In the same way as the proof of Theorem
3.3
,
it holds that under
\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{mN^{2\gamma}}\to 0
and

\frac{n^{1-\alpha+\epsilon}}{M_{1}^{2\epsilon}\land M_{2}^{2\epsilon}}\to 0
,

(5.62)

\check{\sigma}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\sigma^{*})^{2},

and that under
\frac{n^{2-\alpha}}{mN^{2\gamma}}\to 0
and

\frac{n^{2-\alpha+\epsilon}}{M_{1}^{2\epsilon}\land M_{2}^{2\epsilon}}\to 0
,

(5.63)

\sqrt{n}(\check{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\longrightarrow}}N(0,2(\sigma^{*})^{4}).

(1) We obtain from (
5.62
) and Theorem
3.5
that

\check{\theta}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\theta_{1}^{*}
,
\check{\eta}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\eta_{1}^{*}
and

\check{\theta}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\theta_{2}^{*}
.

(2) Since it follows from Theorem
3.5
that
under

\frac{n^{2-\alpha}}{mN^{2\gamma}}\to 0
and

\frac{n^{2-\alpha+\epsilon}}{M_{1}^{2\epsilon}\land M_{2}^{2\epsilon}}\to 0
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\bigl(\check{s}^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}-(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{m^{1/2}N^{\gamma}}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}\bigl(s^{*}+u(\check{s}-s^{*})\bigr)^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}-1}\mathrm{d}u\,m^{1/2}N^{\gamma}(\check{s}-s^{*})

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\bigl(\check{\kappa}\check{s}^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}-\kappa^{*}(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\check{\kappa}\sqrt{n}\bigl(\check{s}^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}-(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\bigr)+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{m^{1/2}N^{\gamma}}(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}m^{1/2}N^{\gamma}(\check{\kappa}-\kappa^{*})

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\bigl(\check{\eta}\check{s}^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}-\eta^{*}(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\check{\eta}\sqrt{n}\bigl(\check{s}^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}-(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\bigr)+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{m^{1/2}N^{\gamma}}(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}m^{1/2}N^{\gamma}(\check{\eta}-\eta^{*})

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\bigl(\check{\sigma}^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}-(\sigma^{*})^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\sigma^{*})^{\frac{2\alpha}{1-\alpha}}}{1-\alpha}\sqrt{n}(\check{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})+o_{p}(1),

one has that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\check{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\bigl(\check{\sigma}^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}\check{s}^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}-(\sigma^{*})^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\bigl)

\displaystyle=\check{\sigma}^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}\sqrt{n}\bigl(\check{s}^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}-(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\bigl)+(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\sqrt{n}\bigl(\check{\sigma}^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}-(\sigma^{*})^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}\bigl)

\displaystyle=(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\frac{(\sigma^{*})^{\frac{2\alpha}{1-\alpha}}}{1-\alpha}\sqrt{n}(\check{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})+o_{p}(1)

(5.64)

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{2}^{*}}{(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\sqrt{n}(\check{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})+o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\check{\theta}_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\bigl(\check{\sigma}^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}\check{\kappa}\check{s}^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}-(\sigma^{*})^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}\kappa^{*}(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\bigl)

\displaystyle=\kappa^{*}(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\frac{(\sigma^{*})^{\frac{2\alpha}{1-\alpha}}}{1-\alpha}\sqrt{n}(\check{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})+o_{p}(1)

(5.65)

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{1}^{*}}{(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\sqrt{n}(\check{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})+o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\check{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\bigl(\check{\sigma}^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}\check{\eta}\check{s}^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}-(\sigma^{*})^{\frac{2}{1-\alpha}}\eta^{*}(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\bigl)

\displaystyle=\eta^{*}(s^{*})^{\frac{-1}{1-\alpha}}\frac{(\sigma^{*})^{\frac{2\alpha}{1-\alpha}}}{1-\alpha}\sqrt{n}(\check{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})+o_{p}(1)

(5.66)

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{1}^{*}}{(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\sqrt{n}(\check{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})+o_{p}(1),

and therefore, we obtain from (
5.63
) that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\check{\theta}_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\\
\check{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}^{*}\\
\check{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{*}\\
\check{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\theta_{1}^{*}\\
\eta_{1}^{*}\\
\theta_{2}^{*}\\
(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}\end{pmatrix}\sqrt{n}(\check{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{\nu}_{-1}^{*}}{(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\sqrt{n}(\check{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\longrightarrow}}N\biggl(0,\frac{2}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{-1}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{-1}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}\biggr)=N(0,K).

(b) As in the proof of Theorem
3.3
,
it holds that under
\frac{n^{1-\alpha}}{mN^{2\gamma}}\to 0
and

\frac{n^{1-\alpha+\epsilon}}{M_{1}^{2\epsilon}\land M_{2}^{2\epsilon}}\to 0
,

(5.67)

(\bar{\tau}_{1,1}^{2},\bar{\tau}_{1,2}^{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2}),

and that under
\frac{n^{2-\alpha}}{mN^{2\gamma}}\to 0
and

\frac{n^{2-\alpha+\epsilon}}{M_{1}^{2\epsilon}\land M_{2}^{2\epsilon}}\to 0
,

(5.68)

\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\tau}_{1,1}^{2}-(\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2}\\
\bar{\tau}_{1,2}^{2}-(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\longrightarrow}}N\Biggl(0,\begin{pmatrix}2(\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{4}&0\\
0&2(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{4}\end{pmatrix}\Biggr).

(1) It is clear from (
5.67
) and Theorem
3.5

that the estimators are consistent.

(2) Let
\boldsymbol{\nu}^{*}=(0,\boldsymbol{\nu}_{-1}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}
and

\Phi(x,y)=3\pi^{2}(x^{1/\alpha}y^{-1/\alpha}-1)^{-1},\quad\Psi(x,y)=\{3\pi^{2}(y^{-1/\alpha}-x^{-1/\alpha})^{-1}\}^{\alpha}.

It then follows that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\bar{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\bigl\{\Phi(\bar{\tau}_{1,1}^{2},\bar{\tau}_{1,2}^{2})-\Phi((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\partial\Phi((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2})\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\tau}_{1,1}^{2}-(\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2}\\
\bar{\tau}_{1,2}^{2}-(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\end{pmatrix}+o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\bar{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\bigl\{\Psi(\bar{\tau}_{1,1}^{2},\bar{\tau}_{1,2}^{2})-\Psi((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\partial\Psi((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2})\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\tau}_{1,1}^{2}-(\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2}\\
\bar{\tau}_{1,2}^{2}-(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\end{pmatrix}+o_{p}(1).

In the same way as (
5.64
)-(
5.66
), it holds that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\bar{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{*})

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{2}^{*}}{(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\sqrt{n}(\bar{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})+o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\bar{\theta}_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*})

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{1}^{*}}{(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\sqrt{n}(\bar{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})+o_{p}(1),

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\bar{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}^{*})

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{1}^{*}}{(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\sqrt{n}(\bar{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2})+o_{p}(1).

Therefore, one has from (
5.68
)
that under
\frac{n^{2-\alpha}}{mN^{2\gamma}}\to 0
and

\frac{n^{2-\alpha+\epsilon}}{M_{1}^{2\epsilon}\land M_{2}^{2\epsilon}}\to 0
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}^{*}\\
\bar{\theta}_{1}-\theta_{1}^{*}\\
\bar{\eta}_{1}-\eta_{1}^{*}\\
\bar{\theta}_{2}-\theta_{2}^{*}\\
\bar{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{*})^{2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\biggl\{\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\partial\Phi((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2})+\boldsymbol{\nu}^{*}\frac{\partial\Psi((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2})}{(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\biggr\}\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\tau}_{1,1}^{2}-(\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2}\\
\bar{\tau}_{1,2}^{2}-(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\end{pmatrix}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=:B\sqrt{n}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\tau}_{1,1}^{2}-(\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2}\\
\bar{\tau}_{1,2}^{2}-(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\end{pmatrix}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\longrightarrow}}N(0,L),

where

L=B\begin{pmatrix}2(\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{4}&0\\
0&2(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{4}\end{pmatrix}B^{\mathsf{T}}.

Since

\partial\Phi(\tau_{1,1}^{2},\tau_{1,2}^{2})=\frac{\mu_{1,1}\mu_{1,2}}{3\pi^{2}\alpha\sigma^{2}}(-\mu_{1,1}^{\alpha},\mu_{1,2}^{\alpha}),\quad\partial\Psi(\tau_{1,1}^{2},\tau_{1,2}^{2})=\frac{1}{3\pi^{2}}(-\mu_{1,1}^{1+\alpha},\mu_{1,2}^{1+\alpha})

and
\tau_{k,\ell}^{4}=\sigma^{4}\mu_{k,\ell}^{-2\alpha}
,
it follows that

\partial\Phi((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2})\begin{pmatrix}2(\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{4}&0\\
0&2(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{4}\end{pmatrix}\partial\Phi((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2})^{\mathsf{T}}=\frac{4(\mu_{1,1}^{*})^{2}(\mu_{1,2}^{*})^{2}}{9\pi^{4}\alpha^{2}}=\frac{2d_{1}}{9\pi^{4}},

\partial\Phi((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2})\begin{pmatrix}2(\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{4}&0\\
0&2(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{4}\end{pmatrix}\frac{\partial\Psi((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2})^{\mathsf{T}}}{(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}}=\frac{2\mu_{1,1}^{*}\mu_{1,2}^{*}(\mu_{1,1}^{*}+\mu_{1,2}^{*})}{9\pi^{4}\alpha(1-\alpha)}=\frac{2d_{2}}{9\pi^{4}},

\frac{\partial\Psi((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2})}{(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}2(\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{4}&0\\
0&2(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{4}\end{pmatrix}\frac{\partial\Psi((\tau_{1,1}^{*})^{2},(\tau_{1,2}^{*})^{2})^{\mathsf{T}}}{(1-\alpha)(\sigma^{*})^{2}}=\frac{2\{(\mu_{1,1}^{*})^{2}+(\mu_{1,2}^{*})^{2}\}}{9\pi^{4}(1-\alpha)^{2}}=\frac{2d_{3}}{9\pi^{4}}.

Hence, it holds that

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{9\pi^{4}}\Bigl(d_{1}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}+d_{2}\bigl\{\boldsymbol{e}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\nu}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}+\boldsymbol{\nu}^{*}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}\bigr\}+d_{3}\boldsymbol{\nu}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\nu}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{9\pi^{4}}\begin{pmatrix}d_{1}&d_{2}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{-1}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}\\
d_{2}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{-1}^{*}&d_{3}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{-1}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{-1}^{*})^{\mathsf{T}}\end{pmatrix}.

∎

.

characteristic of
\eta=\eta_{1}/\theta_{2}

\eta
affects the variation of the sample path when
z
is varied.
Figures
7
-
9
are the sample paths, where
\eta
is changed.

Figure
7
is the sample path with
\theta=(0,0.2,1.5,0.2,1)
and
\eta=7.5
.
Figure
7
shows that if
\eta
is large, the variation of the sample path
is large near
z=0
and small near
z=1
.
